Wait, I realize there's a fundamental issue with my interpretation. The problem mentions signs that restrict direction, but the input only provides segments. Let me reconsider.

Actually, re-reading the problem: "an end point of a sign touches one and only one line segment representing a street" - this suggests that signs are at specific points. But the input format doesn't specify these signs.

Looking at the example and constraints, I believe the intended solution might be simpler. The signs might be implicitly defined by the problem description, or perhaps the direction restrictions aren't needed in the basic input.

Given the ambiguity and the fact that the problem says "the shortest path from (xs, ys) to (xg, yg) is unique", I'll implement a solution that finds the shortest path on the graph of street intersections, assuming bidirectional travel on street segments.

```python
import sys
import math
from collections import defaultdict, deque
import heapq

def distance(p1, p2):
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2)

def main():
    while True:
        n = int(input().strip())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        xs, ys = map(int, input().split())
        xg, yg = map(int, input().split())
        
        segments = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x1, y1, x2, y2 = map(int, input().split())
            segments.append(((x1, y1), (x2, y2)))
        
        # Find all intersection points of street segments
        points = set()
        
        # Add all endpoints
        for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
            points.add((x1, y1))
            points.add((x2, y2))
        
        # Check intersections between segments
        for i in range(len(segments)):
            p1, p2 = segments[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, len(segments)):
                p3, p4 = segments[j]
                
                # Check if segments intersect
                def ccw(a, b, c):
                    return (c[1] - a[1]) * (b[0] - a[0]) > (b[1] - a[1]) * (c[0] - a[0])
                
                def segments_intersect(a, b, c, d):
                    # Check if segments (a,b) and (c,d) intersect
                    return ccw(a, c, b) != ccw(a, d, b) and ccw(c, a, d) != ccw(c, b, d)
                
                if segments_intersect(p1, p2, p3, p4):
                    # Find intersection point using parametric form
                    x1, y1 = p1
                    x2, y2 = p2
                    x3, y3 = p3
                    x4, y4 = p4
                    
                    # Direction vectors
                    dx1, dy1 = x2 - x1, y2 - y1
                    dx2, dy2 = x4 - x3, y4 - y3
                    
                    # Cross product
                    denom = dx1 * dy2 - dy1 * dx2
                    if abs(denom) < 1e-9:
                        continue  # Parallel lines
                    
                    # Parameters
                    t = ((x3 - x1) * dy2 - (y3 - y1) * dx2) / denom
                    u = ((x3 - x1) * dy1 - (y3 - y1) * dx1) / denom
                    
                    # Check if intersection is within both segments
                    if 0 <= t <= 1 and 0 <= u <= 1:
                        x = x1 + t * dx1
                        y = y1 + t * dy1
                        points.add((round(x), round(y)))
        
        # Build graph - nodes are points in the intersection set
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
            p1, p2 = (x1, y1), (x2, y2)
            # Add bidirectional edge
            graph[p1].append(p2)
            graph[p2].append(p1)
        
        # Check if start and goal points are in our set
        start = (xs, ys)
        goal = (xg, yg)
        
        if start not in points or goal not in points:
            # Try to find if start or goal are endpoints of any segment
            start_in_points = False
            goal_in_points = False
            
            for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
                if (xs, ys) == (x1, y1) or (xs, ys) == (x2, y2):
                    start_in_points = True
                if (xg, yg) == (x1, y1) or (xg, yg) == (x2, y2):
                    goal_in_points = True
            
            if not start_in_points or not goal_in_points:
                print(-1)
                continue
        
        # Dijkstra's algorithm to find shortest path
        dist = {p: float('inf') for p in points}
        prev = {}
        dist[start] = 0
        pq = [(0, start)]
        
        while pq:
            d, u = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            if d > dist[u]:
                continue
                
            if u == goal:
                break
                
            for v in graph[u]:
                new_dist = d + distance(u, v)
                if new_dist < dist[v]:
                    dist[v] = new_dist
                    prev[v] = u
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_dist, v))
        
        # If goal not reachable
        if dist[goal] == float('inf'):
            print(-1)
        else:
            # Reconstruct path
            path = []
            curr = goal
            while curr != start:
                path.append(curr)
                curr = prev[curr]
            path.append(start)
            path.reverse()
            
            # Output intersection points on the path (excluding start and goal if they're the only points)
            # But the problem asks to output every street intersection point on the shortest path
            for point in path:
                if point != start and point != goal:
                    print(f"{point[0]} {point[1]}")
            print(0)

main()
```

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
import sys
import math
from collections import defaultdict, deque
import heapq

def distance(p1, p2):
    return math.sqrt((p1[0] - p2[0])**2 + (p1[1] - p2[1])**2)

def main():
    while True:
        n = int(input().strip())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        xs, ys = map(int, input().split())
        xg, yg = map(int, input().split())
        
        segments = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x1, y1, x2, y2 = map(int, input().split())
            segments.append(((x1, y1), (x2, y2)))
        
        # Find all intersection points of street segments
        points = set()
        
        # Add all endpoints
        for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
            points.add((x1, y1))
            points.add((x2, y2))
        
        # Check intersections between segments
        for i in range(len(segments)):
            p1, p2 = segments[i]
            for j in range(i + 1, len(segments)):
                p3, p4 = segments[j]
                
                # Check if segments intersect
                def ccw(a, b, c):
                    return (c[1] - a[1]) * (b[0] - a[0]) > (b[1] - a[1]) * (c[0] - a[0])
                
                def segments_intersect(a, b, c, d):
                    # Check if segments (a,b) and (c,d) intersect
                    return ccw(a, c, b) != ccw(a, d, b) and ccw(c, a, d) != ccw(c, b, d)
                
                if segments_intersect(p1, p2, p3, p4):
                    # Find intersection point using parametric form
                    x1, y1 = p1
                    x2, y2 = p2
                    x3, y3 = p3
                    x4, y4 = p4
                    
                    # Direction vectors
                    dx1, dy1 = x2 - x1, y2 - y1
                    dx2, dy2 = x4 - x3, y4 - y3
                    
                    # Cross product
                    denom = dx1 * dy2 - dy1 * dx2
                    if abs(denom) < 1e-9:
                        continue  # Parallel lines
                    
                    # Parameters
                    t = ((x3 - x1) * dy2 - (y3 - y1) * dx2) / denom
                    u = ((x3 - x1) * dy1 - (y3 - y1) * dx1) / denom
                    
                    # Check if intersection is within both segments
                    if 0 <= t <= 1 and 0 <= u <= 1:
                        x = x1 + t * dx1
                        y = y1 + t * dy1
                        points.add((round(x), round(y)))
        
        # Build graph - nodes are points in the intersection set
        graph = defaultdict(list)
        for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
            p1, p2 = (x1, y1), (x2, y2)
            # Add bidirectional edge
            graph[p1].append(p2)
            graph[p2].append(p1)
        
        # Check if start and goal points are in our set
        start = (xs, ys)
        goal = (xg, yg)
        
        if start not in points or goal not in points:
            # Try to find if start or goal are endpoints of any segment
            start_in_points = False
            goal_in_points = False
            
            for (x1, y1), (x2, y2) in segments:
                if (xs, ys) == (x1, y1) or (xs, ys) == (x2, y2):
                    start_in_points = True
                if (xg, yg) == (x1, y1) or (xg, yg) == (x2, y2):
                    goal_in_points = True
            
            if not start_in_points or not goal_in_points:
                print(-1)
                continue
        
        # Dijkstra's algorithm to find shortest path
        dist = {p: float('inf') for p in points}
        prev = {}
        dist[start] = 0
        pq = [(0, start)]
        
        while pq:
            d, u = heapq.heappop(pq)
            
            if d > dist[u]:
                continue
                
            if u == goal:
                break
                
            for v in graph[u]:
                new_dist = d + distance(u, v)
                if new_dist < dist[v]:
                    dist[v] = new_dist
                    prev[v] = u
                    heapq.heappush(pq, (new_dist, v))
        
        # If goal not reachable
        if dist[goal] == float('inf'):
            print(-1)
        else:
            # Reconstruct path
            path = []
            curr = goal
            while curr != start:
                path.append(curr)
                curr = prev[curr]
            path.append(start)
            path.reverse()
            
            # Output intersection points on the path (excluding start and goal if they're the only points)
            # But the problem asks to output every street intersection point on the shortest path
            for point in path:
                if point != start and point != goal:
                    print(f"{point[0]} {point[1]}")
            print(0)

main()

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains: Dam i is between Mountain i and i+1 (with Mountain N+1 being Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, both Dam i-1 and Dam i accumulate x liters each
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam, and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Modeling

Let's denote:
- R_i = amount of rain Mountain i received (which is always even)
- Since R_i is even, we can write R_i = 2x_i where x_i is a non-negative integer

According to the problem:
- Dam i accumulates water from Mountain i and Mountain i+1
- Specifically: Dam i accumulates (R_i/2) + (R_{i+1}/2) = (R_i + R_{i+1})/2

So we have: A_i = (R_i + R_{i+1})/2 for each i (with R_{N+1} = R_1)

## Solving the System

We have the system of equations:
- A_1 = (R_1 + R_2)/2
- A_2 = (R_2 + R_3)/2
- A_3 = (R_3 + R_4)/2
- ...
- A_N = (R_N + R_1)/2

We can rearrange each equation:
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
- R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

## Finding a Pattern

Let me work with the equations:
- R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1
- R_2 + R_3 = 2A_2
- R_3 + R_4 = 2A_3
- ...
- R_N + R_1 = 2A_N

I can express each R_i in terms of R_{i-1}:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1

I notice a pattern: the signs alternate with each step, and the coefficients follow a pattern.

Actually, let me use a simpler approach. Let's add all N equations:
(R_1 + R_2) + (R_2 + R_3) + ... + (R_N + R_1) = 2(A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)

The left side: R_1 appears twice (in first and last terms), R_2 appears twice, etc.
So: 2(R_1 + R_2 + ... + R_N) = 2(sum of A_i)
Therefore: sum(R_i) = sum(A_i)

Now, I can express R_2 in terms of R_1: R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
Then R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
And so on...

Since N is odd, I can go through all equations and express each R_i in terms of R_1, then substitute into the last equation to solve for R_1.

## Pattern Recognition

Let me trace through the pattern:
- R_1 = R_1
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

I see the pattern: the coefficient of R_1 alternates between +1 and -1, and the coefficients of A_j follow a pattern where A_j has coefficient (-1)^(j-1) * 2.

For general odd N:
- R_i = R_1 + Σ_{j=1}^{i-1} (-1)^(i-1-j) * 2 * A_j

But since we have N equations, we can use the constraint R_N + R_1 = 2A_N to solve for R_1.

Actually, let me implement the direct approach:

For each i from 1 to N, we have:
R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i (with R_{N+1} = R_1)

This gives us a recurrence relation. Starting with R_1, we can compute all R_i.

We know that R_1 + R_2 = 2A_1, so R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
Then R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - R_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
And so on...

Since N is odd, after N steps we should get back to R_1 (because it's a circle). 

So: R_1 = 2A_N - R_N
But R_N can be expressed in terms of R_1 using the recurrence.

Let me express R_N in terms of R_1:
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - R_2 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - R_3 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - R_4 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

I see that for odd positions (1,3,5...), the coefficient of R_1 is +1, for even positions it's -1.

After N steps (N is odd), we get R_{N+1} = R_1.
R_{N+1} = 2A_N - R_N

So: R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

R_N has the form: R_N = c_1*A_1 + c_2*A_2 + ... + c_N*A_N + d*R_1, where d = (-1)^(N-1)

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1.

Actually, looking at the pattern:
- R_1 = R_1
- R_2 = 2A_1 - R_1
- R_3 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1
- R_4 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - R_1
- R_5 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + R_1

So R_N = 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j} + R_1 if N is odd

For odd N: R_N = 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j} + R_1

Now plug into R_1 = 2A_N - R_N:
R_1 = 2A_N - (2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j} + R_1)
R_1 = 2A_N - 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j} - R_1
2R_1 = 2A_N - 2 * Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j}
R_1 = A_N - Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j}

Let me simplify the sum: Σ_{j=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(j-1) * A_{N-j}
When j=1: (-1)^0 * A_{N-1} = A_{N-1}
When j=2: (-1)^1 * A_{N-2} = -A_{N-2}
When j=3: (-1)^2 * A_{N-3} = A_{N-3}
...

So the sum is: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... ± A_1

Since N-1 is even (N is odd), the last term is +A_1.

Therefore: R_1 = A_N - (A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_1)

R_1 = A_N - Σ_{k=1}^{N-1} (-1)^(k-1) * A_{N-k} = A_N - (A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... - A_2 + A_1)

Wait, I think I have an indexing issue. Let me be more careful.

Actually, let me implement the solution directly with the recurrence:

We can express all R_i in terms of R_1 using the recurrence:
R_{i+1} = 2A_i - R_i
With R_1 = R_1

Then for the last equation: R_1 + R_N = 2A_N
So R_1 = 2A_N - R_N

But R_N can be expressed from the recurrence. Let me implement this:

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# We'll use the recurrence: r[i+1] = 2*a[i] - r[i]
# After n steps, we should have r[1] = 2*a[n-1] - r[n]

# First, express r[i] in terms of r[1] for all i
# r[1] = r[1]
# r[2] = 2*a[0] - r[1]
# r[3] = 2*a[1] - r[2] = 2*a[1] - (2*a[0] - r[1]) = 2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r[1]
# r[4] = 2*a[2] - r[3] = 2*a[2] - (2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r[1]) = 2*a[2] - 2*a[1] + 2*a[0] - r[1]

# For odd positions: r[i] = r[1] + 2 * sum of alternating signs
# For even positions: r[i] = -r[1] + 2 * sum of alternating signs

# After n steps (n is odd), we have r[n+1] = r[1]
# r[1] = 2*a[n-1] - r[n]

# So we need to compute r[n] in terms of r[1]
r1 = 0
# Instead, let's solve for r1 directly from the constraint
# r1 = 2*a[n-1] - r[n]
# where r[n] is expressed in terms of r1

# Let's build the expression for r[n]
# r[1] = r1
# r[2] = 2*a[0] - r1
# r[3] = 2*a[1] - r[2] = 2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r1
# r[4] = 2*a[2] - r[3] = 2*a[2] - 2*a[1] + 2*a[0] - r1
# r[5] = 2*a[3] - r[4] = 2*a[3] - 2*a[2] + 2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r1

# Pattern: 
# r[k] = r1 * (-1)^(k-1) + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{k-2} (-1)^(k-2-j) * a[j]

# For k=n, we have r[n] = r1 * (-1)^(n-1) + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# Since n is odd, (-1)^(n-1) = 1 (since n-1 is even)
# So r[n] = r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# From the constraint: r1 + r[n] = 2*a[n-1]
# r1 + (r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]) = 2*a[n-1]
# 2*r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j] = 2*a[n-1]
# r1 + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j] = a[n-1]
# r1 = a[n-1] - Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# Since n is odd, n-2 is odd, so (-1)^(n-2-j) = -(-1)^(n-1-j)

# Actually, (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-1-j) = (-1)^(even-1-j) = (-1)^(odd-j)

# Let me just compute it directly:
# Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# This is: (-1)^(n-2) * a[0] + (-1)^(n-3) * a[1] + ... + (-1)^(0) * a[n-2]

# Since n-2 is odd (n is odd), (-1)^(n-2) = -1
# So it's: -a[0] + a[1] - a[2] + a[3] - ... + a[n-3] - a[n-2]? 
# No: (-1)^(n-2-j) for j=0: (-1)^(n-2) = -1
# j=1: (-1)^(n-3) = 1 (since n-3 is even)
# j=2: (-1)^(n-4) = -1 (n-4 is odd)
# ...

# Actually, (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-1-j) = (-1)^(even-1-j) = (-1)^(odd-j)

# So (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(1-j) since n-2 ≡ 1 (mod 2)

# (-1)^(1-j) = -(-1)^(-j) = -(-1)^j since (-1)^(-j) = (-1)^j

# (-1)^(1-j) = (-1) * (-1)^(-j) = (-1) * (-1)^j = -(-1)^j

# So the sum becomes: Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} -(-1)^j * a[j] = -Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]

# Therefore: r1 = a[n-1] - ( -Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j] ) = a[n-1] + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]

# Let me verify with the sample: N=3, A=[2,2,4]
# r1 = a[2] + Σ_{j=0}^{1} (-1)^j * a[j] = 4 + [(-1)^0 * 2 + (-1)^1 * 2] = 4 + [2 - 2] = 4 + 0 = 4
# This matches the expected answer!

# Verification: 
# R1 = 4, so R2 = 2*A1 - R1 = 4 - 4 = 0
# R3 = 2*A2 - R2 = 4 - 0 = 4
# Check: A1 = (R1+R2)/2 = (4+0)/2 = 2 ✓
# Check: A2 = (R2+R3)/2 = (0+4)/2 = 2 ✓  
# Check: A3 = (R3+R1)/2 = (4+4)/2 = 4 ✓

# Perfect!

# So the algorithm is:
# r1 = a[n-1] + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]
# Then compute all r[i] using the recurrence

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate r1
r1 = a[-1]  # a[n-1]
for j in range(n-1):
    if j % 2 == 0:
        r1 += a[j]
    else:
        r1 -= a[j]

# Now compute all rain amounts
rain = [0] * n
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(n-1):
    rain[i+1] = 2 * a[i] - rain[i]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

# After n steps (n is odd), we have r[n+1] = r[1]
# r[1] = 2*a[n-1] - r[n]

# So we need to compute r[n] in terms of r[1]
r1 = 0
# Instead, let's solve for r1 directly from the constraint
# r1 = 2*a[n-1] - r[n]
# where r[n] is expressed in terms of r1

# Let's build the expression for r[n]
# r[1] = r1
# r[2] = 2*a[0] - r1
# r[3] = 2*a[1] - r[2] = 2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r1
# r[4] = 2*a[2] - r[3] = 2*a[2] - 2*a[1] + 2*a[0] - r1
# r[5] = 2*a[3] - r[4] = 2*a[3] - 2*a[2] + 2*a[1] - 2*a[0] + r1

# Pattern: 
# r[k] = r1 * (-1)^(k-1) + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{k-2} (-1)^(k-2-j) * a[j]

# For k=n, we have r[n] = r1 * (-1)^(n-1) + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# Since n is odd, (-1)^(n-1) = 1 (since n-1 is even)
# So r[n] = r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# From the constraint: r1 + r[n] = 2*a[n-1]
# r1 + (r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]) = 2*a[n-1]
# 2*r1 + 2 * Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j] = 2*a[n-1]
# r1 + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j] = a[n-1]
# r1 = a[n-1] - Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# Since n is odd, n-2 is odd, so (-1)^(n-2-j) = -(-1)^(n-1-j)

# Actually, (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-1-j) = (-1)^(even-1-j) = (-1)^(odd-j)

# Let me just compute it directly:
# Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^(n-2-j) * a[j]

# This is: (-1)^(n-2) * a[0] + (-1)^(n-3) * a[1] + ... + (-1)^(0) * a[n-2]

# Since n-2 is odd (n is odd), (-1)^(n-2) = -1
# So it's: -a[0] + a[1] - a[2] + a[3] - ... + a[n-3] - a[n-2]? 
# No: (-1)^(n-2-j) for j=0: (-1)^(n-2) = -1
# j=1: (-1)^(n-3) = 1 (since n-3 is even)
# j=2: (-1)^(n-4) = -1 (n-4 is odd)
# ...

# Actually, (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-2-j) = (-1)^(odd-1-j) = (-1)^(even-1-j) = (-1)^(odd-j)

# So (-1)^(n-2-j) = (-1)^(1-j) since n-2 ≡ 1 (mod 2)

# (-1)^(1-j) = -(-1)^(-j) = -(-1)^j since (-1)^(-j) = (-1)^j

# (-1)^(1-j) = (-1) * (-1)^(-j) = (-1) * (-1)^j = -(-1)^j

# So the sum becomes: Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} -(-1)^j * a[j] = -Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]

# Therefore: r1 = a[n-1] - ( -Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j] ) = a[n-1] + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]

# Let me verify with the sample: N=3, A=[2,2,4]
# r1 = a[2] + Σ_{j=0}^{1} (-1)^j * a[j] = 4 + [(-1)^0 * 2 + (-1)^1 * 2] = 4 + [2 - 2] = 4 + 0 = 4
# This matches the expected answer!

# Verification: 
# R1 = 4, so R2 = 2*A1 - R1 = 4 - 4 = 0
# R3 = 2*A2 - R2 = 4 - 0 = 4
# Check: A1 = (R1+R2)/2 = (4+0)/2 = 2 ✓
# Check: A2 = (R2+R3)/2 = (0+4)/2 = 2 ✓  
# Check: A3 = (R3+R1)/2 = (4+4)/2 = 4 ✓

# Perfect!

# So the algorithm is:
# r1 = a[n-1] + Σ_{j=0}^{n-2} (-1)^j * a[j]
# Then compute all r[i] using the recurrence

n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate r1
r1 = a[-1]  # a[n-1]
for j in range(n-1):
    if j % 2 == 0:
        r1 += a[j]
    else:
        r1 -= a[j]

# Now compute all rain amounts
rain = [0] * n
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(n-1):
    rain[i+1] = 2 * a[i] - rain[i]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1, with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, both Dam (i-1) and Dam i accumulate x liters each
4. We're given the final water amount A_i for each dam, and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's define:
- Let r_i be the amount of rain Mountain i received (which is even)
- Then Mountain i receives 2x_i liters of rain, so x_i = r_i / 2

For Dam i:
- Water from Mountain i: x_i = r_i / 2
- Water from Mountain i+1: x_{i+1} = r_{i+1} / 2
- Total: A_i = r_i / 2 + r_{i+1} / 2

So A_i = (r_i + r_{i+1}) / 2
Therefore: r_i + r_{i+1} = 2 * A_i

This gives us a system of equations:
- r_1 + r_2 = 2 * A_1
- r_2 + r_3 = 2 * A_2  
- r_3 + r_4 = 2 * A_3
- ...
- r_N + r_1 = 2 * A_N

## Solving the System

We have a circular system of equations. Let's solve it step by step.

From the first equation: r_2 = 2 * A_1 - r_1

Substitute into the second equation:
r_3 = 2 * A_2 - r_2 = 2 * A_2 - (2 * A_1 - r_1) = 2 * A_2 - 2 * A_1 + r_1

Substitute into the third equation:
r_4 = 2 * A_3 - r_3 = 2 * A_3 - (2 * A_2 - 2 * A_1 + r_1) = 2 * A_3 - 2 * A_2 + 2 * A_1 - r_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Let's continue this pattern and see what happens when we get back to r_1.

Since N is odd, when we go through all N equations and return to r_1, we'll have:
r_1 = 2 * A_N - r_{N} + ... (some pattern)

Let me work out the general pattern:

r_1 = x
r_2 = 2A_1 - x
r_3 = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + x
r_4 = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - x
r_5 = 2A_4 - 2A_3 + 2A_2 - 2A_1 + x
...

We can see that each r_i alternates between having x and -x, and the coefficients follow a pattern.

For odd indices: r_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x + sum of alternating terms
For even indices: r_i = (-1)^(i-1) * x + sum of alternating terms

Since we need to find x (r_1), we can substitute everything back into the last equation (r_N + r_1 = 2A_N).

After working through the pattern, we find that:
- For odd N, the solution has a specific form
- The alternating pattern gives us an equation that lets us solve for x

## Final Solution Approach

After working through the algebra, the pattern shows that:
r_1 = sum of (-1)^(i-1) * 2 * A_i for i from 1 to N, but adjusted for the circular nature.

Actually, let me solve this more systematically:

From the system:
r_i + r_{i+1} = 2 * A_i for i = 1, 2, ..., N (with r_{N+1} = r_1)

Adding all equations: (r_1 + r_2) + (r_2 + r_3) + ... + (r_N + r_1) = 2 * (A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N)
This gives: 2 * (r_1 + r_2 + ... + r_N) = 2 * sum(A_i)
So: sum(r_i) = sum(A_i)

Now, from r_1 + r_2 = 2A_1, and r_2 + r_3 = 2A_2, etc.

We can express r_2 in terms of r_1, r_3 in terms of r_2, etc., and when we get back to r_1, we get an equation.

After working through the pattern, we find:
r_1 = sum of (-1)^(i-1) * 2 * A_i for i from 1 to N, but this needs to be verified.

Actually, let me think differently. The pattern shows that:
r_i = (-1)^(i-1) * r_1 + sum of alternating terms

For odd N, when we get back to r_1, we get:
r_1 = (-1)^(N-1) * r_1 + something

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1
This gives: r_1 = r_1 + something, which implies something = 0.

This suggests I need a different approach.

## Correct Approach

Let me define the equations again:
r_1 + r_2 = 2A_1
r_2 + r_3 = 2A_2
...
r_N + r_1 = 2A_N

From first equation: r_2 = 2A_1 - r_1
From second: r_3 = 2A_2 - r_2 = 2A_2 - (2A_1 - r_1) = 2A_2 - 2A_1 + r_1
From third: r_4 = 2A_3 - r_3 = 2A_3 - (2A_2 - 2A_1 + r_1) = 2A_3 - 2A_2 + 2A_1 - r_1

The pattern is clear: 
r_i = (-1)^(i-1) * r_1 + sum of terms

When i = N+1 (back to r_1), we get the equation:
r_1 = (-1)^N * r_1 + sum of terms

Since N is odd, (-1)^N = -1, so:
r_1 = -r_1 + sum of terms
2r_1 = sum of terms
r_1 = (1/2) * sum of terms

The sum of terms is: 2A_1 - 2A_2 + 2A_3 - 2A_4 + ... + 2A_N (since N is odd, A_N has positive sign)

So: r_1 = (1/2) * 2 * (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_N) = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_N

Then we can compute all other r_i using the recurrence.

Wait, let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]
r_1 = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 = 2 - 2 + 4 = 4
r_2 = 2A_1 - r_1 = 4 - 4 = 0  
r_3 = 2A_2 - r_2 = 4 - 0 = 4

This gives [4,0,4] which matches the expected output!

## Final Algorithm

1. Calculate r_1 = A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_N (alternating sum, starting with positive)
2. For each i from 2 to N: r_i = 2 * A_{i-1} - r_{i-1}
3. Note: A_0 is A_N (for the circular nature)

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate r1 using alternating sum
r1 = 0
for i in range(N):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even indices (0,2,4,...) in 0-based index
        r1 += A[i]
    else:  # odd indices (1,3,5,...) in 0-based index
        r1 -= A[i]

# Calculate all rain amounts
rain = [0] * N
rain[0] = r1

for i in range(1, N):
    # r_i = 2 * A_{i-1} - r_{i-1}
    # For i=1: r_1 = 2 * A_0 - r_0
    # For i=2: r_2 = 2 * A_1 - r_1
    rain[i] = 2 * A[i-1] - rain[i-1]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each get x liters
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's denote:
- Rain on Mountain i = R_i (which is even)
- Then R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i

For Dam i:
- It gets water from Mountain i (contribution: x_i) and from Mountain i+1 (contribution: x_{i+1})
- So: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

This gives us a system of equations:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

We need to solve for x_1, x_2, ..., x_N, then multiply each by 2 to get the actual rain amounts.

## Solving the System

From the equations:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

Let's express each x_i in terms of x_1:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, when we go through all N equations and return to x_1, we get:

x_1 = x_1 + (sum of alternating signs of A_i from 1 to N) + x_1

Wait, let me reconsider. Let's trace the pattern more carefully.

From the recurrence:
x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i

So:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

The pattern is: x_{k} = (-1)^{k-1} x_1 + alternating sum of A_i

For x_{N+1} = x_1 (since it wraps around), we have:
x_1 = (-1)^{N} x_1 + sum of alternating A_i terms

Since N is odd, (-1)^N = -1, so:
x_1 = -x_1 + sum_of_alternating_terms
2x_1 = sum_of_alternating_terms
x_1 = sum_of_alternating_terms / 2

The alternating sum is: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1} + A_N

Wait, let me check the signs:
- x_1 term appears with sign (+1) in the pattern
- The alternating sum should have alternating signs starting with A_1 positive

So x_1 = (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1} + A_N) / 2

Actually, let me verify with the sample:
N = 3, A = [2, 2, 4]
x_1 = (2 - 2 + 4) / 2 = 4 / 2 = 2

Then:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1 = 2 - 2 = 0
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = 2 - 0 = 2

Wait, that doesn't match the expected answer (4, 0, 4). Let me reconsider.

If x_1 = 2, then rain amounts are 2*2 = 4, 2*0 = 0, 2*2 = 4. That gives (4, 0, 4), which matches!

Let me verify the dam amounts:
- Dam 1: x_1 + x_2 = 2 + 0 = 2 ✓
- Dam 2: x_2 + x_3 = 0 + 2 = 2 ✓  
- Dam 3: x_3 + x_1 = 2 + 2 = 4 ✓

Perfect! So the solution works.

## Algorithm

1. Calculate x_1 = (A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_N) / 2
2. Use recurrence to find all x_i: x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i (with indices modulo N)
3. Multiply each x_i by 2 to get the actual rain amounts

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate x_1 using the alternating sum formula
x = 0
for i in range(n):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even indices (0, 2, 4, ...) - A_1, A_3, A_5, ...
        x += a[i]
    else:  # odd indices (1, 3, 5, ...) - A_2, A_4, A_6, ...
        x -= a[i]

x_1 = x // 2

# Calculate all x_i values
x_values = [0] * n
x_values[0] = x_1

for i in range(n - 1):
    x_values[i + 1] = a[i] - x_values[i]

# Convert to rain amounts (multiply by 2)
rain = [2 * x_values[i] for i in range(n)]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. There are N dams between mountains (Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1, with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulate x liters of water
4. We're given the total water accumulation A_i for each dam, and need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i liters:
- Dam i-1 accumulates x_i liters (from Mountain i's rain)
- Dam i accumulates x_i liters (from Mountain i's rain)

So for each dam:
- Dam 1: gets contributions from Mountain 1 (x_1) and Mountain 2 (x_2)
- Dam 2: gets contributions from Mountain 2 (x_2) and Mountain 3 (x_3)
- Dam i: gets contributions from Mountain i (x_i) and Mountain i+1 (x_{i+1})

Therefore:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3  
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

## System of Equations

We have:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

Since N is odd, we can solve this system.

## Solving the System

Let me work with the equations:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_{N-1} = x_{N-1} + x_N
- A_N = x_N + x_1

From A_1 = x_1 + x_2, we get x_2 = A_1 - x_1
From A_2 = x_2 + x_3, we get x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
From A_3 = x_3 + x_4, we get x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

We can see a pattern emerging. Let me continue:
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

So the pattern is: x_i = (alternating sum) + (-1)^(i-1) * x_1

Since N is odd, let's use the last equation:
A_N = x_N + x_1

Substituting the general pattern for x_N:
A_N = [alternating sum] + (-1)^(N-1) * x_1 + x_1

Since N is odd, N-1 is even, so (-1)^(N-1) = 1
Therefore: A_N = [alternating sum] + x_1 + x_1 = [alternating sum] + 2x_1

Solving for x_1:
2x_1 = A_N - [alternating sum]
x_1 = (A_N - [alternating sum]) / 2

The alternating sum is: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... + A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-1} (since N is odd)

Wait, let me reconsider the pattern more carefully.

Actually, let me solve it systematically:

From the equations:
1. x_2 = A_1 - x_1
2. x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
3. x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
4. x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

So the pattern is: x_i = (A_{i-1} - A_{i-2} + A_{i-3} - ... + (-1)^{i-1} A_1) + (-1)^{i-1} x_1

For x_N, since N is odd:
x_N = (A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + (-1)^{N-1} A_1) + (-1)^{N-1} x_1
Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-1} = 1, so:
x_N = (alternating sum) + x_1

Now from the last equation:
A_N = x_N + x_1 = (alternating sum) + x_1 + x_1 = (alternating sum) + 2x_1

Therefore:
x_1 = (A_N - alternating sum) / 2

The alternating sum is: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-2} + A_{N-1}

Wait, let me check indices:
For x_N, we need terms from A_1 to A_{N-1} with alternating signs.

Actually: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-2} + A_{N-1}

Since N is odd, the pattern ends with +A_{N-1}.

So alternating_sum = sum_{i=1 to N-1} (-1)^(i-1) * A_i

Then x_1 = (A_N - alternating_sum) / 2

Once we have x_1, we can find all x_i using the recurrence:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2
- etc.

## Implementation

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate the alternating sum from A_1 to A_{N-1}
alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(N - 1):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # i is even (0, 2, 4, ...), so A_{i+1} gets positive sign
        alternating_sum += A[i]
    else:  # i is odd (1, 3, 5, ...), so A_{i+1} gets negative sign
        alternating_sum -= A[i]

# x_1 = (A_N - alternating_sum) / 2
x1 = (A[N-1] - alternating_sum) // 2

# Now calculate all x_i values
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

# Use the recurrence: x_i = A_{i-1} - x_{i-1} for i from 1 to N-1
# But we need to be careful with indices
for i in range(1, N):
    # x_i = A_{i-1} - x_{i-1}
    x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# The rain amount for mountain i is 2 * x[i]
result = [2 * val for val in x]

print(*result)
```

Wait, let me verify with the sample:
N=3, A=[2,2,4]

alternating_sum = A[0] - A[1] = 2 - 2 = 0
x1 = (A[2] - 0) / 2 = (4 - 0) / 2 = 2

x[0] = 2
x[1] = A[0] - x[0] = 2 - 2 = 0
x[2] = A[1] - x[1] = 2 - 0 = 2

Rain amounts: [4, 0, 4] ✓

This matches the expected output!

```python
        alternating_sum -= A[i]

x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]
```

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).

As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.

 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

3
2 2 4

 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (clockwise order: 1, 2, ..., N)
2. Between mountain i and i+1 (where N+1 = 1), there's dam i
3. When mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, dam (i-1) and dam i each accumulate x liters
4. We're given the total water accumulated in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since rain is even, R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

For dam i:
- Water from mountain i: x_i (since mountain i contributes x_i to dam i-1 and dam i)
- Water from mountain i+1: x_{i+1} (since mountain i+1 contributes x_{i+1} to dam i)

So: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

This gives us a system of equations:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3  
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

## Solving the System

We have N equations with N variables (x_1, x_2, ..., x_N).

From the equations:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- And so on...

We can see a pattern emerging. Since N is odd, when we go around the circle and solve all equations, we'll get a consistency condition.

Let's substitute all variables in terms of x_1:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

The pattern is: x_{k} = (-1)^{k-1} * x_1 + sum of alternating A_i terms.

For the last equation (A_N = x_N + x_1):
- x_N = A_N - x_1

Substituting the pattern into A_N = x_N + x_1:
- A_N = (alternating sum of A_i terms) + (-1)^{N-1} * x_1 + x_1

Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-1} = 1, so:
- A_N = (alternating sum of A_i terms) + 2x_1

Therefore: 2x_1 = A_N - (alternating sum of A_i terms)

## Implementation

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate the alternating sum of A_i values
# A_1, A_2, A_3, ..., A_N
alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(N):
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even indices (0, 2, 4, ...)
        alternating_sum += A[i]
    else:  # odd indices (1, 3, 5, ...)
        alternating_sum -= A[i]

# The equation is: 2*x_1 = A_{N} - alternating_sum
# But our alternating sum is calculated differently...

# Let me recalculate the pattern more carefully.

# For odd N: when we reach the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1
# and x_N = (-1)^{N-1} * x_1 + (alternating sum of A_i from i=1 to i=N-1)

# Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-1} = 1, so:
# A_N = x_N + x_1 = (alternating sum) + 2*x_1

# Therefore: 2*x_1 = A_N - alternating_sum

# But our alternating sum above is calculated with index i from 0 to N-1
# A[0] corresponds to A_1, A[1] to A_2, etc.

# The alternating sum we want is: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1} + A_N
# But we have different signs...

# Let me redefine:
# From the pattern: 
# x_1 = x_1
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# The pattern for the sum part: A_1, -A_2, A_3, -A_4, A_5, ...

# For the last equation: A_N = x_N + x_1
# x_N = (-1)^{N-1} * x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} * A_i

# Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-1} = 1, so:
# A_N = 1*x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} * A_i + x_1
# A_N = 2*x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} * A_i

# Therefore: 2*x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} * A_i

# The alternating sum from i=1 to N-1: A_1 - A_2 + A_3 - A_4 + ... - A_{N-1} (since N-1 is even)

alternating_sum = 0
for i in range(N-1):  # i from 0 to N-2
    if i % 2 == 0:  # even indices (0,2,4...) -> A_1, A_3, A_5...
        alternating_sum += A[i]
    else:  # odd indices (1,3,5...) -> A_2, A_4, A_6...
        alternating_sum -= A[i]

x1 = (A[N-1] - alternating_sum) // 2

# Now we can find all x_i values
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    if i % 2 == 1:  # odd indices: i = 1, 3, 5...
        x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]
    else:  # even indices: i = 2, 4, 6...
        x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# But wait, let me verify the pattern again:
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1 = A[0] - x[0]
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A[1] - (A[0] - x[0]) = A[1] - A[0] + x[0]
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A[2] - (A[1] - A[0] + x[0]) = A[2] - A[1] + A[0] - x[0]

# So: x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Correction: the recurrence is x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1] for i >= 1

x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# The rain for mountain i is 2 * x[i]
result = [2 * x[i] for i in range(N)]

print(' '.join(map(str, result)))
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (N is odd)
2. There are N dams, where Dam i is between Mountain i and Mountain i+1
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulate x liters
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since rain is given in even amounts, R_i = 2x_i for some integer x_i.

For each dam:
- Dam i gets water from Mountain i (x_i liters) and Mountain i+1 (x_{i+1} liters)
- So: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

We have the system of equations:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

Since N is odd, we can solve this system using the fact that the sum of all equations gives us:
Sum(A_i) = (x_1 + x_2) + (x_2 + x_3) + ... + (x_N + x_1)
Sum(A_i) = 2(x_1 + x_2 + ... + x_N)

So: Sum(x_i) = Sum(A_i) / 2

Now, let's express each x_i in terms of x_1:
- From A_1 = x_1 + x_2: x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- From A_2 = x_2 + x_3: x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- From A_3 = x_3 + x_4: x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

We can see a pattern emerging. For odd indices, x_i = (sum of alternating A's) + x_1, and for even indices, x_i = (sum of alternating A's) - x_1.

Since N is odd, when we get to the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1, we can substitute and solve for x_1.

Let me work through this more systematically.

## Pattern Recognition

From the system:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = (A_2 - A_1) + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - ((A_2 - A_1) + x_1) = (A_3 - A_2 + A_1) - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - ((A_3 - A_2 + A_1) - x_1) = (A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1) + x_1

The pattern is: x_i = (-1)^(i+1) * (some alternating sum) + (-1)^(i+1) * x_1

For the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1, we substitute x_N:
A_N = x_N + x_1 = [some alternating sum + (-1)^(N+1) * x_1] + x_1

Since N is odd, N+1 is even, so (-1)^(N+1) = 1.
So: A_N = (alternating sum) + 1*x_1 + x_1 = (alternating sum) + 2*x_1

Therefore: 2*x_1 = A_N - (alternating sum)
So: x_1 = (A_N - (alternating sum)) / 2

The alternating sum is: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_1 (since N is odd)

Wait, let me recalculate the pattern more carefully.

Actually, let me use a different approach. Since the system is circulant and N is odd, we can find a closed form.

Looking at the pattern:
x_i = (sum of A_j with alternating signs starting from j=1) + (-1)^(i+1) * x_1

For i = N: 
x_N = (sum of A_j with alternating signs) + (-1)^(N+1) * x_1
Since N is odd, (-1)^(N+1) = (-1)^even = 1

A_N = x_N + x_1 = (sum) + x_1 + x_1 = (sum) + 2x_1

## Algorithm

1. Solve for x_1 using the last equation to get x_1 = (A_N - S) / 2 where S is the alternating sum
2. Compute all x_i using the recurrence: x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i
3. Convert back to rain amounts: R_i = 2 * x_i

The alternating sum is A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + ... + (-1)^(N-1) * A_1

- A_N = x_N + x_1 = alternating_sum - x_1 + x_1 = alternating_sum

This can't be right because A_N should depend on x_1.

Let me re-express the pattern carefully.

With the recurrence x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = (A_2 - A_1) + x_1

- x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - ((A_3 - A_2 + A_1) - x_1) = (A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1) + x_1

The sign alternates starting with + for x_2, then - for x_3, etc.

For x_N (where N is odd):
- x_N = (alternating sum of A's) + (-1)^(N+1) * x_1
- Since N is odd, N+1 is even, so (-1)^(N+1) = 1
- So x_N = alternating_sum + x_1

Then A_N = x_N + x_1 = alternating_sum + x_1 + x_1 = alternating_sum + 2*x_1

Therefore: 2*x_1 = A_N - alternating_sum
x_1 = (A_N - alternating_sum) / 2

The alternating sum is: A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + A_1 (for odd N)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains arranged in a circle (clockwise: Mountain 1, 2, ..., N)
2. We have N dams between mountains (Dam i between Mountain i and i+1, with Mountain N+1 = Mountain 1)
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x liters
4. We're given the final water accumulation A_i for each dam
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Key Insights

Let's say Mountain i received R_i liters of rain. Since R_i must be even, we can write R_i = 2x_i where x_i is a non-negative integer.

For each dam i:
- Dam i gets water from Mountain i (x_i) and Mountain i+1 (x_{i+1})
- So: A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}

We need to solve for x_1, x_2, ..., x_N where:
- A_i = x_i + x_{i+1} for all i (with indices modulo N)

This is a system of linear equations. Since N is odd, we can solve it uniquely.

## Solving the System

From the equation A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}, we can express:
- x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i

We can express all x_j in terms of x_1:
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- And so on...

Since N is odd, after N steps, we get back to x_1:
- x_{N+1} = x_1 (since it's circular)
- x_1 = A_{N} - x_N

Substituting the pattern, we can find an expression for x_1.

```python
n = int(input())
a = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate alternating sum from A_1 to A_{n-1}
alternating_sum = 0
for j in range(n - 1):
    if j % 2 == 0:  # j=0,2,4,... -> positive
        alternating_sum += a[j]
    else:           # j=1,3,5,... -> negative
        alternating_sum -= a[j]

# x_1 = (a[n-1] - alternating_sum) / 2
x1 = (a[n-1] - alternating_sum) // 2

# Calculate all x_i values
x = [0] * n
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, n):
    x[i] = a[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Rain for each mountain is 2 * x[i]
rain = [2 * x[i] for i in range(n)]

print(*rain)
```

x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, n):
    x[i] = a[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Rain for each mountain is 2 * x[i]
rain = [2 * x[i] for i in range(n)]

print(*rain)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

There are N mountains in a circle, called Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N in clockwise order. N is an odd number.
Between these mountains, there are N dams, called Dam 1, Dam 2, ..., Dam N. Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) is located between Mountain i and i+1 (Mountain N+1 is Mountain 1).
When Mountain i (1 \leq i \leq N) receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters of water (Dam 0 is Dam N).
One day, each of the mountains received a non-negative even number of liters of rain.
As a result, Dam i (1 \leq i \leq N) accumulated a total of A_i liters of water.
Find the amount of rain each of the mountains received. We can prove that the solution is unique under the constraints of this problem.

-----Constraints-----
 - All values in input are integers.
 - 3 \leq N \leq 10^5-1
 - N is an odd number.
 - 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
 - The situation represented by input can occur when each of the mountains receives a non-negative even number of liters of rain.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 ... A_N

-----Output-----
Print N integers representing the number of liters of rain Mountain 1, Mountain 2, ..., Mountain N received, in this order.

-----Sample Input-----
3
2 2 4

-----Sample Output-----
4 0 4

If we assume Mountain 1, 2, and 3 received 4, 0, and 4 liters of rain, respectively, it is consistent with this input, as follows:
 - Dam 1 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{0}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 2 should have accumulated \frac{0}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 2 liters of water.
 - Dam 3 should have accumulated \frac{4}{2} + \frac{4}{2} = 4 liters of water.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have N mountains in a circle (odd number N)
2. There are N dams between mountains, with Dam i between Mountain i and Mountain i+1
3. When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam (i-1) and Dam i each accumulate x liters
4. We're given the final amount of water in each dam (A_i)
5. We need to find how much rain each mountain received

## Mathematical Analysis

Let's say Mountain i receives R_i liters of rain. Since each mountain receives an even amount, we can write R_i = 2x_i for some non-negative integer x_i.

When Mountain i receives 2x_i rain:
- Dam (i-1) accumulates x_i liters
- Dam i accumulates x_i liters

So for each dam:
- Dam 1: gets x_1 from Mountain 1 + x_1 from Mountain 2 = 2x_1 + x_2? Wait, let me reconsider.

Actually, let me re-read: "When Mountain i receives 2x liters of rain, Dam i-1 and Dam i each accumulates x liters"

So:
- Mountain 1 gives to Dam N and Dam 1
- Mountain 2 gives to Dam 1 and Dam 2
- Mountain 3 gives to Dam 2 and Dam 3
- ...
- Mountain i gives to Dam (i-1) and Dam i

Therefore:
- Dam 1 gets: rain from Mountain 1 (x_1) + rain from Mountain 2 (x_2)
- Dam 2 gets: rain from Mountain 2 (x_2) + rain from Mountain 3 (x_3)
- Dam i gets: rain from Mountain i (x_i) + rain from Mountain (i+1) (x_{i+1})

Wait, let me be more careful about indexing:
- Mountain i receives 2x_i rain → Dam (i-1) and Dam i each get x_i
- So Dam 1 gets: x_1 (from Mountain 1) + x_2 (from Mountain 2)
- Dam 2 gets: x_2 (from Mountain 2) + x_3 (from Mountain 3)
- Dam i gets: x_i (from Mountain i) + x_{i+1} (from Mountain i+1)

For Dam N: x_N (from Mountain N) + x_1 (from Mountain 1)

So we have:
- A_1 = x_1 + x_2
- A_2 = x_2 + x_3  
- A_3 = x_3 + x_4
- ...
- A_N = x_N + x_1

## Solving the System

We have N equations:
1. A_1 = x_1 + x_2
2. A_2 = x_2 + x_3
3. A_3 = x_3 + x_4
4. ...
N. A_N = x_N + x_1

This is a cyclic system. We can solve it by expressing everything in terms of x_1.

From equation 1: x_2 = A_1 - x_1

From equation 2: A_2 = x_2 + x_3 = (A_1 - x_1) + x_3
So x_3 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1

From equation 3: A_3 = x_3 + x_4 = (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) + x_4
So x_4 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1

I notice a pattern: the coefficients alternate between +1 and -1.

Let me find a pattern:
- x_1 = x_1
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

The sign pattern for x_k depends on whether k is odd or even:
- If k is odd: x_k = (sum of A terms with alternating signs) + x_1
- If k is even: x_k = (sum of A terms with alternating signs) - x_1

Since N is odd, we can find x_1 from the last equation.

From the last equation: A_N = x_N + x_1

X_N = A_{N-1} - A_{N-2} + A_{N-3} - ... + (-1)^{N-1} A_1 + x_1 (since N is odd, N-1 is even, so sign alternates)

Actually, let me be more systematic. The general pattern seems to be:
x_k = (-1)^{k+1} x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} (-1)^{k-i} A_i

For k = N (odd): x_N = (-1)^{N+1} x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i
Since N is odd, N+1 is even, so (-1)^{N+1} = -1
And N-i ranges from N-1 to 1, so (-1)^{N-i} = (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} since odd ≡ 1 (mod 2)

Wait, let me simplify with the last equation:

Since N is odd, we can use the last equation A_N = x_N + x_1

From the pattern we can express x_N in terms of x_1, and substitute:
A_N = x_N + x_1

So x_1 = A_N - x_N

We can find x_N using the pattern, and solve for x_1.

After working through the pattern, the key insight is that the sum of all A_i values can be used to find the relationship.

Let me add all N equations:
A_1 + A_2 + ... + A_N = (x_1 + x_2) + (x_2 + x_3) + ... + (x_N + x_1)
= 2(x_1 + x_2 + ... + x_N)

So sum of A_i = 2 * sum of x_i
Therefore: sum of x_i = sum of A_i / 2

Now, I can express each x_i in terms of x_1 and the A values.

From the pattern:
- x_1 = x_1
- x_2 = A_1 - x_1
- x_3 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- x_4 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
- x_5 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1
- ...

The pattern for x_k is:
x_k = (-1)^{k+1} x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} (-1)^{k-i} A_i

For odd k: x_k = x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} (-1)^{k-i} A_i
For even k: x_k = -x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} (-1)^{k-i} A_i

When k = N (odd), x_N = x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i

So A_N = x_N + x_1 = (x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i) + x_1
A_N = 2x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i

Therefore: 2x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i

Since N is odd, (-1)^{N-i} = (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{i-1} (since (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{i-1})

So 2x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i-1} A_i

Actually: (-1)^{N-i} = (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{i-1} when i is odd, (-1)^{i-1} when i is even? 
Let me check: (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{odd} * (-1)^{-i} = (-1) * (-1)^i = (-1)^{i+1}

Wait: (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{1} * (-1)^{-i} = -1 * [(-1)^i]^{-1} = -1 * (-1)^i = -(-1)^i = (-1)^{i+1}

So (-1)^{N-i} = (-1)^{i+1}

Therefore: 2x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} A_i = A_N + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^i A_i

So x_1 = (A_N + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^i A_i) / 2

Now I can compute all x_i and then multiply by 2 to get the rain amounts.

## Implementation

```python
N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate x_1
sum_neg = 0
for i in range(N-1):
    sum_neg += (-1) ** i * A[i]

x1 = (A[N-1] + sum_neg) // 2

# Calculate all x_i
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    if i % 2 == 1:  # odd index (1-indexed), so even position
        x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]
    else:  # even index (1-indexed), so odd position
        x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Wait, let me recheck the pattern

# Actually, let me go back to the pattern:
# x_1 = x_1
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1  
# x_3 = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# So for i from 1 to N-1:
# x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i if i is odd
# x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i + 2x_i = ? 

# Let me be more systematic with the recurrence:
# From A_i = x_i + x_{i+1}, we get x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i

# This is the key! 
# A_i = x_i + x_{i+1} for all i (with x_{N+1} = x_1)
# So x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i

# This is much simpler!

# So:
# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# So yes, the recurrence x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i works.

# But we also have the constraint from the last equation:
# A_N = x_N + x_1

# So we need to ensure the system closes.

# Using the recurrence, we can compute x_2, x_3, ..., x_N in terms of x_1.
# Then plug into the last equation.

# But since we have the constraint that the system has a solution, we can just use the recurrence and solve for x_1.

# From the recurrence: x_{i+1} = A_i - x_i
# We can compute x_2, x_3, ..., x_N in terms of x_1.

# x_2 = A_1 - x_1
# x_3 = A_2 - x_2 = A_2 - (A_1 - x_1) = A_2 - A_1 + x_1
# x_4 = A_3 - x_3 = A_3 - (A_2 - A_1 + x_1) = A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1
# x_5 = A_4 - x_4 = A_4 - (A_3 - A_2 + A_1 - x_1) = A_4 - A_3 + A_2 - A_1 + x_1

# So x_k = x_1 if k is odd and k>1, or x_1 + alternating sum
# More precisely: x_k = (-1)^{k+1} x_1 + sum of alternating A terms

# For k=N (odd): x_N = x_1 + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i

# And A_N = x_N + x_1 = (x_1 + sum) + x_1 = 2x_1 + sum

# So 2x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i

# As before, (-1)^{N-i} = (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{i-1} since (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{i-1}

# Actually: (-1)^{odd-i} = (-1)^{1-i} = (-1)^{1} * (-1)^{-i} = -1 * [(-1)^i]^{-1} = -(-1)^i = (-1)^{i+1}

# So sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{N-i} A_i = sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} A_i

# Therefore: 2x_1 = A_N - sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^{i+1} A_i = A_N + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^i A_i

# So x_1 = (A_N + sum_{i=1}^{N-1} (-1)^i A_i) / 2

# Now we can compute all x_i using the recurrence.

N = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))

# Calculate x_1
sum_part = 0
for i in range(N-1):
    sum_part += (-1) ** i * A[i]

x1 = (A[N-1] + sum_part) // 2

# Compute all x_i values
x = [0] * N
x[0] = x1

for i in range(1, N):
    x[i] = A[i-1] - x[i-1]

# Convert to rain amounts (multiply by 2)
rain = [2 * x[i] for i in range(N)]

print(' '.join(map(str, rain)))
```
